- `necklaces`: Necklaces — every binary necklace of a length n (5 to 10; rings equal after turning count once) drawn once as a ring of n beads, filled beads big and bright and empty beads small and dim, with the beads of a non-periodic (Lyndon) necklace brighter and higher; 8, 14, 20, 36, 60 and 108 necklaces, of which 6, 9, 18, 30, 56 and 99 are Lyndon; each ring is drawn from its least rotation turned at random; pale blue on a dark navy-black ground; seed % 6 picks n and ⌊seed / 6⌋ the order of the panels and the turns; an exactly periodic pattern; a schematic picture; tests read every ring back from the picture and check that its least rotation gives exactly the enumeration written separately (no repeats or gaps), that the Lyndon brightness agrees with a periodicity test written separately and the count with the formula, and compare point by point the ink, height, tone and occlusion of the beads; the current catalog has 1140 kinds, 1141 gallery choices including plain.

- `youngdiagrams`: Young diagrams — every partition of an integer n (6 to 11) drawn once as a Young diagram (English notation), the tone and height of each cell following its hook length; 11, 15, 22, 30, 42 and 56 partitions; amber squares on a dark navy-black ground; seed % 6 picks n and ⌊seed / 6⌋ the order of the panels; an exactly periodic pattern; a schematic picture; tests read every diagram and the hook length of every cell back from the picture, check that they are exactly the enumeration written separately in the test (all partitions and hook lengths) with no repeats or gaps, that the number n!/∏h of standard Young tableaux of each diagram is an integer and that the squares sum to exactly n!, that groups of seeds change only the order, and compare point by point the ink, height, tone and occlusion; the current catalog has 1139 kinds, 1140 gallery choices including plain.

- `dyckpaths`: Dyck paths — every Dyck path (or Motzkin path) of a length drawn once in a small panel: Dyck paths of length 8, 10 and 12 (the Catalan numbers 14, 42 and 132) and Motzkin paths of length 5, 6 and 7 (the Motzkin numbers 21, 51 and 127); the polyline of the path and a dim dot at every lattice point a path can reach; gold on a dark navy-black ground; seed % 6 picks the variant and ⌊seed / 6⌋ the order of the panels; an exactly periodic pattern; a schematic picture; tests read the path of every panel back from the picture and check that they are exactly the enumeration written separately in the test (no repeats and no gaps) and that their number matches the formulas of the Catalan and Motzkin numbers, that groups of seeds change only the order, and compare point by point the ink, height, tone and occlusion of the lines and dots; the current catalog has 1138 kinds, 1139 gallery choices including plain.

- `projectiveplane`: Projective plane — the point-line incidence matrix of a finite projective plane of order q (3, 4, 5, 7, 8 or 9): v = q² + q + 1 points and lines, q + 1 points on every line, any two lines meeting in exactly one point and any two points on exactly one line; it comes from the circulant matrix of a Singer perfect difference set, which the seed can multiply by a unit, shift and shuffle in rows and columns; mint-green dots on a dark navy-black ground; seed % 6 picks the order and ⌊seed / 6⌋ the multiplier, the shift and whether to shuffle; an exactly periodic pattern; a schematic picture that covers only the cyclic Singer type, related to but unlike the ±1 matrices `paleytable` and `hadamardmatrix`, `cayleytable` and `perfectarray`; tests read the matrix back from the picture and check q + 1 dots in every row and column, v(q + 1) in all, exactly one common dot in any two rows and any two columns, that an unshuffled matrix is circulant with a perfect difference set in its first row, and compare point by point the ink, height, tone and occlusion; the current catalog has 1137 kinds, 1138 gallery choices including plain.

- `costasambiguity`: Costas ambiguity — the displacement-count picture of a Welch Costas array (f(i) = g^(i + c) mod p − 1, primes 11 to 29): every cell (dx, dy) shows the number of pairs of marks with j − i = dx and f(j) − f(i) = dy, with n pairs in the middle (0, 0) (the largest, brightest dot) and at most one for every other displacement (all displacement vectors are different); amber dots on a dark navy-black ground; seed % 6 picks the prime and ⌊seed / 6⌋ the primitive root and the shift; an exactly periodic pattern; a schematic picture that uses only the Welch construction, related to but unlike `perfectarray` (a torus autocorrelation, where this is an aperiodic ambiguity function); tests read the counts back from the picture and check the n(n − 1) cells with one pair, that there is none with two, the one peak in the middle, the point symmetry and n − |d| cells for each dx and dy, find a permutation from the picture by depth-first search and check that it is a Welch array of the prime, and compare point by point the ink, height, tone and occlusion; the current catalog has 1136 kinds, 1137 gallery choices including plain.

- `magicsquares`: Magic squares — every cell holds a number, brighter and higher the larger it is, and every row, column and both diagonals have the same sum; the Siamese method for the orders 5, 7 and 9, the diagonal-complement method for 8 and 12, and the Strachey method for 10; gold on a dark teal ground; seed % 6 picks the variant and ⌊seed / 6⌋ one of the eight turns and flips and whether to complement (n → n² + 1 − n); an exactly periodic pattern; a schematic picture that uses only these three methods, related to but unlike `cayleytable` and `graecolatin` (the property is equal sums over rows, columns and diagonals, not a Latin square); tests read the number of every cell back from the height, check that it is a permutation of 1 to n², that rows, columns and both diagonals have the same sum and that the order 10 is not associative, check with a Siamese method and a diagonal-complement method written again that turns, flips and complements of them give the result, and compare point by point the ink, height, tone and occlusion; the current catalog has 1135 kinds, 1136 gallery choices including plain.

- `trominotiling`: Tromino tiling — the classic recursive tiling of a board with one cell missing by L-shaped trominoes: an L in the middle covers the cell next to the centre in each of the three quadrants without the missing cell, then the four quadrants are tiled the same way; the three cells of a piece join without a seam, pieces (and the missing cell) are parted by gaps, the level of a piece is drawn by a hash and sets its height and tone; gold on a dark blue-black ground; seed % 6 picks a side of 8, 16 or 32 cells and the missing cell anywhere or in one of the four middle cells, and ⌊seed / 6⌋ the missing cell and the levels; an exactly periodic pattern; a schematic picture that gives only the one tiling of that recursion; tests read the missing cell and the joins between cells back from the picture, check that every piece is an L, that the number of pieces is (n² − 1)/3 and that nothing is joined across the edge of the tile, check that the cells of one piece have the same height and tone, and compare point by point the ink, height, tone and occlusion; the current catalog has 1134 kinds, 1135 gallery choices including plain.

- `celticinterlace`: Celtic interlace — a 16×16 torus of square tiles, each a crossing (a horizontal and a vertical band cross) or a pair of quarter-circle turns, every tile edge crossed by exactly one band at its middle; bands go over and under alternately at crossings (the parity of column plus row says which is over), so that any two consecutive crossings along a band are one over and one under, and the band under stops one gap short of the band over, with the height map giving the interlaced relief; gold on a dark brown-black ground; the seed picks 6 variants (turn probability 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, each drawn tile by tile or mirror-symmetric); an exactly periodic pattern; a schematic picture, a random interlace and not a designed traditional knot, related to but unlike `truchettiles` and `hextruchettiles` (which have no crossings and no over/under); tests read the tile types and over/under back from the picture, compare point by point with an ideal figure of predicates (ink, level, tone and occlusion), follow every closed band with a tracing written separately and check the alternation and the joins at tile edges, and check the symmetry of the mirror variants and the number of turn tiles; the current catalog has 1133 kinds, 1134 gallery choices including plain.

- `wireworldcircuits`: WireWorld circuits — snapshots of the WireWorld cellular automaton on a random lattice of wires on a 64×64 torus (empty, conductor, electron head and tail; a conductor with exactly 1 or 2 heads among its 8 neighbours becomes a head); teal squares on near-black; seed % 6 picks the wire probability (0.33, 0.42) and the number of steps (30, 31, 90) and ⌊seed / 6⌋ the lattice of wires and the start of the electrons, so seeds 0 to 5 are one circuit at different numbers of steps; an exactly periodic pattern; a schematic picture, not a designed logic circuit, a snapshot cellular automaton like `excitableautomata` but with another rule and structure; tests read the cell states back from the picture and check, with a rule written again in the test, snapshots one step and 60 steps apart, that the wires lie on the lattice only and that the wires of the sparser circuit are among those of the denser one, and compare point by point the tone, height and occlusion in the cells and in the gaps; the current catalog has 1132 kinds, 1133 gallery choices including plain.

- `excitableautomata`: Excitable automata — snapshots of three-state (resting, excited, refractory) Brian's Brain (a cell fires with exactly 2 of 8 neighbours excited) and Greenberg–Hastings (at least 1 of 4 neighbours excited) on a 64×64 torus; amber squares on near-black navy; seed % 6 picks the rule and the number of steps (40, 41, 100, 101, 60, 61) and ⌊seed / 6⌋ the random start, so seeds 0 to 5 are one start at different numbers of steps; an exactly periodic pattern; a schematic picture, related to but unlike `cyclicwaves` (where a cell advances only when a neighbour holds the next state); tests read the cell states back from the picture and check, with a rule written again in the test, snapshots one step and 60 steps apart, and compare point by point the tone, height and occlusion in the cells and in the gaps; the current catalog has 1131 kinds, 1132 gallery choices including plain.

- `sizeillusions`: Size illusions — the Ebbinghaus illusion (equal central discs, one among large discs and the other among small ones; also a 2×2 lattice and a pair whose central discs clearly differ), the Delboeuf illusion (equal discs in a close and in a far ring) and the Kanizsa triangle and square (pac-men whose mouths face the middle, so that a figure is seen with no edge drawn); ivory on near-black; the seed picks one of 6 variants; an exactly periodic pattern whose geometry is exact and whose perception is not measured; a schematic picture, unlike `opticalillusions`, `lineillusions` and `contrastillusions` this one is about size and contours; tests read the picture back only and compare point by point, away from the edges, with an ideal figure drawn separately in the test by predicates (including the occlusion), measure from the picture the widths of the central discs and the sizes of the discs and rings round them, and check the angles and directions of the mouths of the pac-men and that there is no ink on the edges of the illusory figure; the current catalog has 1130 kinds, 1131 gallery choices including plain.

- `randomdotstereogram`: Random-dot stereogram — the left half of the tile is a field of random round dots and the right half is the same field shifted by a disparity (the right cell (i, j) holds the dot of the left cell (i + d, j), d being not 0 only inside a hidden shape), so that the shape floats in front of or behind the plane when the two eyes fuse the halves; pale white dots on near-black; the seed picks one of 6 variants (a disc, a square, a ring, a diamond, a ramp and two discs); an exactly periodic pattern; alone it is only noise, and I did not check it by fusing with my eyes (only by a stereo match in a program); a schematic picture, not compared with the existing dot or halftone kinds; tests read the picture back only (the dots are inverted from the height) and check that the right field is the left field shifted by the disparity in every cell, that a stereo match that knows nothing about the shape finds the displacement and not 0 in the shape, the density and lack of pattern of the dots, and their round bump shape; the current catalog has 1129 kinds, 1130 gallery choices including plain.

- `contrastillusions`: Contrast illusions — simultaneous contrast (grey squares of one value on dark and light bands, and on a cosine ramp), the White illusion (grey patches of one value inside black and white bars), the Cornsweet illusion (a light and a dark ramp on the two sides of each edge, with plateaus of one value far away), a Mach staircase and Adelson's checker shadow (a light square inside the shadow having the value of a dark one outside it); neutral greys; the seed picks one of 6 variants; an exactly periodic pattern whose geometry and values are exact and whose perception is not measured; a schematic picture, unlike `opticalillusions` and `lineillusions` this one is about brightness; tests read the picture back only and compare point by point, away from the edges, with an ideal pattern drawn separately in the test by predicates and closed forms (including the occlusion), check directly that the squares, patches and checks that should have one value do while their backgrounds differ, the polarity of the Cornsweet ramps and their plateaus, and the level of each step of the staircase; the current catalog has 1128 kinds, 1129 gallery choices including plain.

- `lineillusions`: Line illusions — the Zöllner illusion (ticks on parallel long lines, the tilt turned over from line to line), the Ehrenstein illusion (rays round points, leaving an empty centre), the Müller-Lyer illusion (shafts of one length with inward arrowheads or outward tails), the Hering illusion (two horizontal lines across rays) and the Ponzo illusion (two converging lines with two bars of one length); thin ivory lines on near-black; the seed picks one of 6 variants; an exactly periodic pattern whose geometry is exact and whose perception is not measured; a schematic picture, unlike `opticalillusions` (whole areas of light and dark), this one draws thin figures along segments; tests read the picture back only (the ink is inverted from the height) and compare point by point with segments generated separately in the test whose union is taken by brute force, and check the positions of the Zöllner lines and the angles of their ticks, the angles of the Ehrenstein rays and their empty centre, the lengths of the Müller-Lyer rows and the direction of their fins, and that the Hering lines are straight and the Ponzo bars of one length; the current catalog has 1127 kinds, 1128 gallery choices including plain.

- `opticalillusions`: Optical illusions — the café wall (8 rows of black and white squares, each row shifted by 1/2, 1/4 or 3/4 of a square, with grey mortar lines that are in fact straight and level but look sloped), the Hermann grid (black squares parted by light lanes, with grey ghosts at the crossings) and the scintillating grid (with white discs on the crossings too); ivory on near-black; the seed picks one of 6 variants; an exactly periodic pattern whose geometry is exact and whose perception is not measured; a schematic picture, not compared one by one with the existing checkerboard or grid kinds; tests read the picture back only and compare point by point, away from the edges, with an ideal pattern drawn separately in the test by plain predicates, check the positions of the edges of every row of the café wall and the straightness of the mortar lines, the positions and widths of the lanes, and the area mean (1 − (1 − width)² for the Hermann grids); the current catalog has 1126 kinds, 1127 gallery choices including plain.

- `gerstnerocean`: Gerstner ocean — plane waves in several directions added together, where the water particles move horizontally along the wave as well as up and down, so the crests are sharp, with foam where the Jacobian of the displacement is small (where the crests are squeezed); pale cyan-white on a deep sea blue; the seed picks one of 6 variants (a single swell, a wind sea, a cross sea, a choppy sea, a storm and a swell with chop, the wave tables being data in the preset); an exactly periodic pattern of integer wave vectors, a snapshot of one instant; a schematic picture, unlike `raindropripples` (the concentric ripples of raindrops added together) and `kelvinwake` (the far-field crest lines behind a ship), this is a directional sea surface whose displacement sharpens the crests and brings foam; tests read the picture back only (the level is inverted from the height) and compare point by point with a forward map written separately in the test (from the rest position to the displaced position and height, then sampled), check the foam and the tone with a Jacobian by central differences, and check the single swell against Kepler's equation solved by bisection; the current catalog has 1125 kinds, 1126 gallery choices including plain.

- `islamicstars`: Islamic stars — star patterns on tilings of regular polygons by Hankin's polygons-in-contact method: in every polygon two rays leave the midpoint of each edge towards the two neighbouring vertices at the contact angle to the edge, the rays of neighbouring edges meet on the bisector of the vertex, and the stars join into one strapwork; dark gold straps on a deep teal-blue; the seed picks one of 6 variants (4.8.8 at 45°, 60° and 75°, 4.4.4.4 at 60° and 30°, and the snub square tiling 3.3.4.3.4 at 70°); an exactly periodic pattern over a whole number of periods of the tiling, without over-and-under weaving; a schematic picture, unlike `kagome` (a fixed lattice of honeycomb segments) and `wallpapergroups` (a small hook motif repeated under 12 plane groups), this pattern is computed from a tiling and a contact angle; only tilings on a square lattice are used; tests read the picture back only (the ink is inverted from the height) and compare point by point with a model written separately in the test (the ray is intersected with the bisector of the vertex and the union is taken over all segments by brute force), and check that the four straps through the midpoint of an octagon edge lie at 90° ± θ and 270° ± θ, and the four-fold rotation and mirror symmetries; the current catalog has 1124 kinds, 1125 gallery choices including plain.

- `fresneldiffraction`: Fresnel diffraction — the near-field intensity of a plane wave of wavelength 1 behind an opening in a screen on the (x, z) plane (x across, z down as the distance from the screen): at the shadow boundary of a straight edge the intensity is always 1/4 and the first maximum on the lit side is 1.3704, while slits and strips show dense Fresnel fringes at the top that widen and merge going down; every pixel takes the amplitude from the Fresnel integrals C and S (a power series and a continued fraction) at the two ends of each opening; pale violet on a dark blue-violet; the seed picks one of 6 variants (a straight edge, single slits of width 1.6 and 3.2, opaque strips of width 1.6 and 0.6, and a double slit); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture, unlike `talbotcarpet` (the self-imaging of a periodic grating), `cornuspirals` (which draws the Cornu spiral as a curve) and `zoneplate` (the rings of a zone plate), this draws the intensity field behind one edge, slit or strip; tests read the picture back only (the tone is inverted from the height) and compare with C and S integrated by Simpson's rule written separately in the test, and check the 1/4 at the edge's shadow boundary, the first maximum 1.3704 (from memory, checked numerically), the scaling (x, z) → (2x, 4z) and the left-right symmetry; the current catalog has 1123 kinds, 1124 gallery choices including plain.

- `zetalandscape`: Zeta landscape — bands of the phase (cos(4·arg ζ)) and the modulus (one band for every doubling of ln|ζ|) of the Riemann zeta function ζ(s) on the complex plane s = σ + i t, going dark where |ζ| is small, so every zero is a dark core with pinwheel fringes and the pole at s = 1 a set of concentric rings; every pixel is evaluated by Euler–Maclaurin; pale blue on a dark navy; the seed picks one of 6 square windows (with the pole and the trivial zeros, with the first non-trivial zero, with several zeros of the critical line, and so on); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture (only bands of the phase and modulus), unlike `phasevortices` (the phase picture of one specific periodic complex field; this draws the Riemann zeta function itself); the tests read only the drawing back (the tone is recovered from the height) and find ζ by entirely different methods written separately in the test — the Euler product for σ ≥ 2, the Euler transform of the alternating series of η in the critical strip and the functional equation (with Γ by the Lanczos approximation) for σ ≤ −1.5 — and also check the exact values on the real axis (π²/6, π⁴/90, −1/2, −1/12 and so on), the darkness at the trivial zeros and at the first six non-trivial zeros, and the conjugate symmetry; **the standard C1 sweep fails** (the terms of the sum at the left of the windows are as large as 1e15 and the rounding noise exceeds what a second difference tolerates), so there is no C1 check and only the UV period is checked; there is no independent check in the critical strip for t above 16; the window for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas and the ordinates of the zeros are from memory (checked in a prototype) and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 0.2 s in Bun (three runs, not a benchmark). Current catalog: 1122 kinds, 1123 gallery choices including plain.

- `lyapunovfractal`: Lyapunov fractal — the distribution on the (a, b) parameter plane of the Lyapunov exponent of the logistic map x → r·x·(1 − x) driven by a repeating sequence of letters (a for A and b for B): every pixel starts at x = 1/2, skips 60 steps of warm-up and averages ln|r(1 − 2x)| over 120 steps, and the tone is −exponent (bright where the motion is regular, dark in the chaos); amber gold on a dark brown-black; the seed picks one of 6 variants (the windows of the sequences AB, AAB, ABB, AABAB and AABB, and the window of BBBBBBAAAAAA with a from 3.4 to 4 and b from 2.5 to 3.4); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture (a 120-step finite average, not the true Lyapunov exponent), unlike `logisticbifurcation` (the one-dimensional bifurcation diagram of the same map); the tests read only the drawing back (the tone is recovered from the height) and, with another iteration written separately in the test (a different way of writing it and rounding), check the point-by-point agreement where the motion is regular and the mean in the chaos, and check the closed-form exponents ln|2 − r| and ½·ln|4 + 2r − r²| of the stable fixed point and 2-cycle on the diagonal a = b; it is **not C1** (the exponent jumps where the period changes), so there is no C1 check and only the UV period is checked; the variant for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas are from memory and hand calculation and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 0.3 s in Bun (three runs, not a benchmark). Current catalog: 1121 kinds, 1122 gallery choices including plain.

- `standardmap`: Standard map — the finite-time Lyapunov exponent of the Chirikov standard map p′ = p + K·sin x, x′ = x + p′ (on a torus): every pixel is an initial condition followed for 40 steps with the product of the Jacobians, and the tone is the logarithm of the largest singular value of the product over the number of steps (dark in the islands of regular motion, bright in the chaotic sea); the picture fills the whole tile, repeats every 2π in both x and p and is a seamless periodic pattern; jade green on a dark green-black; the seed picks one of 6 kicks K (0.6, 0.9716, 1.2, 1.6, 2.4 and 3.6); a schematic picture (only a 40-step finite-time quantity, not the true Lyapunov exponent), unlike `mapattractors`, `lorenzattractor`, `logisticbifurcation` and `arnoldtongues` (it shows the chaotic and regular regions of a conservative two-dimensional map over the plane of initial conditions); the tests read only the drawing back (the tone is recovered from the height) and, with forward-mode automatic differentiation written separately in the test, check the agreement with the exponent (for K = 3.6 only the mean and the fraction, because the chaos amplifies rounding), the brightness of the hyperbolic and the darkness of the elliptic fixed point, and the reversal symmetry (x, p) → (−x, −p); it is **not C1** (the exponent changes about e^(40λ) times faster with the initial condition), so there is no C1 check and only the UV period is checked; the variant for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas and K ≈ 0.9716 are from memory and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 0.42 s in Bun (three runs, not a benchmark). Current catalog: 1120 kinds, 1121 gallery choices including plain.

- `diffractioncatastrophes`: Diffraction catastrophes — the intensity on the parameter plane of the diffraction integral of a catastrophe, P(x, y) = ∫exp(i(t^(2d) + a·t⁴ + x·t² + y·t))dt: d = 2 is the Pearcey integral of the cusp (two fold caustics y = ±(4|x|/3)·√(|x|/6) meet in the cusp, with a lattice of interference spots inside) and d = 3 a slice of the butterfly for a fixed a; integrated by the trapezoid rule along a ray in the complex plane, pale ice blue on a dark navy; the seed picks one of 6 variants (three windows of the Pearcey integral and the butterfly with a = −2, −1 and −3); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture (a two-dimensional slice of a scalar interference integral, with no physical optical scale), unlike the materials that draw caustics as ray envelopes, `poolcaustics`, `lensarray`, `diffractionpatterns` and `talbotcarpet` (it draws the wave interference fringes near a caustic); the tests read only the drawing back (the intensity is recovered from the height) and, with a Simpson integral along the real axis written separately in the test, check the agreement with the real-axis integral, the mirror symmetry in y and the value (2Γ(5/4))² at the origin, and the outermost fringe lying near the fold caustic; the variant for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas are from memory and hand calculation and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 0.55 s in Bun (three runs, not a benchmark). Current catalog: 1119 kinds, 1120 gallery choices including plain.

- `talbotcarpet`: Talbot carpet — the intensity in the near (Fresnel) field behind a grating of period 1: the field is the sum over the harmonics n of c_n·exp(2πi·n·x)·exp(−2πi·n²·z) (z in Talbot lengths 2d²/λ going down), the picture shows the intensity |field|² (the tone 1 − exp(−intensity)); because the phase 2πn²z is a multiple of 2π at z = 1, the whole tile is exactly one Talbot length high and 3 periods of the grating wide and is seamless along both x and z; amber gold on a dark brown-black; the seed picks one of 6 gratings: amplitude gratings of the open fractions 0.5, 0.25, 0.15 and 0.35 and π-phase gratings of 0.5 and 0.25; a schematic picture (paraxial scalar theory, an ideal thin grating and only 24 harmonics, so there is a Gibbs ripple at the edges), unlike the far-field `diffractionpatterns`, `fourierseries`, `moire` and `zoneplate`; the tests read only the drawing back (the intensity is recovered from the height) and check the conservation of the total power (the same at all depths), the shift by half a period at half a Talbot length and the doubled frequency at a quarter (to 1e-9), that z = 0 is the grating itself, and the agreement with the Fresnel integral of 121 slits in the space domain (amplitude gratings only, bound 0.16); the grating for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle (the model is the same series formula as the sampler); there is no grain and only 6 different pictures (adjacent seeds can pick the same grating, and the salt was adjusted so that seeds 123 and 124 differ); the formulas are from memory and hand calculation and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0007 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 0.2 s in Bun (three runs, not a benchmark). Current catalog: 1118 kinds, 1119 gallery choices including plain.

- `newtonfractal`: Newton fractal — the basins of attraction of the roots of a polynomial under Newton's (or Halley's) iteration: every pixel is a point of the complex plane, the root it reaches decides the tone (ordered by argument) and the number of iterations (made continuous) decides the shading, with fractal petals on the boundaries, rose pink on a dark indigo-black; the seed picks one of 6 variants: z³ − 1, z⁴ − 1, z⁵ − 1, z³ − 2z + 2 (with the attracting cycle 0 ↔ 1, near which 64 steps reach no root and the picture is dark), Halley's method on z³ − 1 and a zoomed boundary of z⁵ − 1; not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture (cut off at 64 steps), unlike the escape-time Mandelbrot and Julia families (it draws basins of roots); the tests read only the drawing back and, with rational maps in closed form and roots in closed form (roots of unity, Cardano's formula) written separately in the test, check the root, count and height/colour point by point, the tones rising with the argument of the roots, the discs round the cycle having no root, and the rotation and mirror symmetry; it is **not C1** (the basin boundaries are discontinuities everywhere), so there is no C1 check and the UV period is checked only to 1e-6; the variant for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas are from memory and hand calculation and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0009 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 0.26 s in Bun (two runs, noisy, not a benchmark). Current catalog: 1117 kinds, 1118 gallery choices including plain.

- `vortexstreet`: Vortex street — the streamlines, in the frame that moves with the street at the speed U = tanh(πh)/2, of two staggered rows of point vortices (the von Kármán vortex street: circulation +1 in the upper row and −1 in the lower, period a = 1, the rows h = ratio·a apart; the stream function of an infinite row of vortices has a closed form in cosh and cos), with cat's-eye rings round every vortex, straight lines far away and bright dots at the vortices, pale green on a dark green-black; the seed picks one of 6 ratios h/a (0.281 the stable street, 0.15, 0.2, 0.4, 0.55 and 0.7); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture (point vortices in an ideal fluid, with no viscosity, shedding or stability analysis); the tests read only the drawing back and, with a direct sum of 801 vortices and a numerically found U written separately in the test, check that the streamlines are where the sum puts them, the periodicity along x and the glide symmetry of the street, and the vortices marked at h/2 above and below the axis; the streamlines near the cores and the stable ratio itself are not tested; the ratio for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas are from memory (compared in a prototype) and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 0.13 s in Bun (two runs, not a benchmark). Current catalog: 1116 kinds, 1117 gallery choices including plain.

- `hydrogenorbitals`: Hydrogen orbitals — the probability density |ψ|² of the electron of a hydrogen atom in a plane through the nucleus (y = 0): the cut at φ = 0 of the real orbital R(n, l; r)·P_l^m(cos θ)·cos(mφ) (with the generalised Laguerre polynomial in R), the density drawn to the power 0.4, so the n − l − 1 radial nodes (circles) and the l − m angular nodes (straight lines through the nucleus, with the z axis too when m > 0) are dark lines, ember orange on a dark brown-black; the seed picks one of 6 orbitals (2s, 3p, 3d, 4p, 4d with m = 1, and 4f); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture (only |ψ|² in one plane, with no phase, spin or relativistic correction); the tests read only the drawing back and, with the three-term recurrence of the Laguerre polynomial and the derivative of the Legendre polynomial written separately in the test, check the dark rings on the radial nodes, the dark cones and axis on the angular nodes and the symmetry under x → −x and z → −z; the relative size of the radial and angular density is not tested; the orbital for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas are from memory (the nodes were checked in a prototype) and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 0.14 s in Bun (one run, not a benchmark). Current catalog: 1115 kinds, 1116 gallery choices including plain.

- `machcone`: Mach cone — the wavefronts of a moving source: the source moves at the Mach number M and emits pulses at fixed intervals, and the circular crests of all the pulses now are drawn (each with the radius of the time since emission, centred where the source was then); for M < 1 the crests are crowded ahead and spread out behind (the Doppler spacing of (1 − M) and (1 + M) periods), at M = 1 all the circles touch at the source, and for M > 1 there are waves only inside the cone behind the source, with the half angle having sin θ = 1/M; yellow-green on a dark olive; the seed picks one of 6 Mach numbers (0, 0.5, 0.8, 1, 1.5 and 2.5); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture (pure geometry of non-dispersive wavefronts in two dimensions, with no shock strength; the amplitude decay and the fade of crowded crests are authored looks); the tests read only the drawing back and, with the geometry of circles and the Doppler spacing written in the test, check that the crests lie on the circles of the pulses emitted k periods ago, the Doppler spacing on the track below the speed of the waves, that it stops at the Mach cone with nothing ahead, and the mirror symmetry about the track; the amplitude decay is not tested; the Mach number for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas are from memory and hand calculation and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 0.1 s in Bun (two runs, not a benchmark). Current catalog: 1114 kinds, 1115 gallery choices including plain.

- `raindropoptics`: Raindrop optics — the ray diagram of parallel sunlight through a unit droplet: 36 rays evenly spaced in the impact parameter enter by the law of refraction, are reflected k times inside by the law of reflection (k = 1 the primary rainbow, 2 the secondary, 3 the tertiary) and refracted out again, with the ray of least deviation, the rainbow ray (137.92° for the primary rainbow of water, n = 1.333, about 42°), drawn brightest; pale amber on a dark teal-blue-black; the seed picks one of 6 droplets (n, k); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture (only the one path of each ray with exactly k reflections, with no Fresnel intensities, no dispersion and no supernumerary bows); the tests read only the drawing back and, with the angle formulas and Descartes's closed form of the least deviation written in the test (the sampler traces with vectors and finds the rainbow ray by a scan), check the path of every ray, that the rainbow ray is brightest and goes in the direction of the rainbow angle, the mirror symmetry about the axis of the sun and that the side away from the sun is empty for one reflection; tones and intensities are not tested; the droplet for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the laws and formulas are from memory (compared in a prototype) and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 0.65 s in Bun (two runs, not a benchmark). Current catalog: 1113 kinds, 1114 gallery choices including plain.

- `hofstadterbutterfly`: Hofstadter butterfly — the energy spectrum of an electron on a two-dimensional square lattice in a magnetic field (Harper's equation): the horizontal axis is the flux α per cell and the vertical axis upward the energy E (−4 to 4); at a rational α = p/q the spectrum is q bands (|P(E)| ≤ 4, P being the trace of the product of q transfer matrices; for even q the two middle bands touch at E = 0), every pixel drawing the bands of the rationals with a denominator up to the limit that lie within 0.003 of the tile (about 1.5 pixels at 512²) of it, with a brightness falling with q, pale blue on a dark blue-black; the seed picks one of 6 windows; not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture (only a finite number of rationals, with no Cantor set for irrational α, no Chern numbers and no density of states); the tests read only the drawing back and, with the eigenvalues of the q×q Hamiltonian found separately in the test by Jacobi rotations, check that it is lit on the bands and dark in the gaps (q up to 7), the mirror symmetry in E → −E and α → 1 − α and that nothing lies above E = 4; the fall of the brightness with q and the bands of larger q are not tested; the C1 sweep checks only the values whose slope is under 100 (the band edges are steep); the window for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas are from memory (compared with the eigenvalues in a prototype) and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 2.0 s in Bun (two runs, not a benchmark; one of the slower ones I have measured). Current catalog: 1112 kinds, 1113 gallery choices including plain.

- `jacobielliptic`: Jacobi elliptic functions — the phase lines (24 at every zero and pole, where arg f is a multiple of π/12) and the fainter modulus ring lines (ln|f| a multiple of 0.6) of sn, cn and dn in the complex plane, the line width corrected by |f′/f|; doubly periodic (the pattern repeating every 2K and 2iK′), the zeros and poles being given by closed forms in K and K′, gold on a dark purple; the seed picks one of 6 (sn with m = 0.5, cn 0.5, dn 0.5, sn 0.8, cn 0.2 and dn 0.8); not a periodic pattern (the plot covers about 1.5 to 3 periods); a schematic picture computed from the theta series (8 terms); the tests read only the drawing back and check, with K and K′ found separately in the test by the arithmetic-geometric mean and the closed-form places of the zeros and poles, the periods, that exactly 24 bright arcs surround every zero and pole (and not the other lattice points) and the mirror symmetry about the real axis; the modulus ring lines and the derivatives of cn and dn have no dedicated test; the C1 sweep uses a step of 1e-8 (at 1e-10 the rounding error of the theta series made a few values exceed the limit); the function for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas are from memory (identities checked with a prototype) and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 0.17 s in Bun (two runs, not a benchmark). Current catalog: 1111 kinds, 1112 gallery choices including plain.

- `kelvinwake`: Kelvin wake — the far-field crest lines of the waves behind a ship (the stationary-phase approximation of deep-water gravity waves): transverse and divergent waves, the two edges of the wedge making the Kelvin angle arcsin(1/3) ≈ 19.47° and no waves outside; the crests are at x = φ·cosθ·(1 + sin²θ), y = φ·sinθ·cos²θ, pale cyan on a dark sea blue-black, the ship being a small bright dot at the middle of the left edge; the seed picks one of 6 wavelengths (3, 4, 5, 6, 8 or 10 transverse wavelengths along the track); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture, not a fluid simulation (only the crest lines of the far-field approximation, with no near field, no hull shape and no Airy smoothing along the Kelvin lines; the amplitude decay and the weight of the divergent waves are authored looks); the tests read only the drawing back and check, with the forward parametrisation written in the test, that the transverse and divergent crests lie on the parametrised curves, that the wake stops at the Kelvin angle, that the transverse crests on the track are one wavelength apart and that it is mirror symmetric about the track; the amplitude and the weight of the divergent waves are not tested; the wavelength for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the derivation is from memory and hand calculation and was not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 0.4 s in Bun (two runs, not a benchmark). Current catalog: 1110 kinds, 1111 gallery choices including plain.

- `arnoldtongues`: Arnold tongues — the mode-locking parameter plane of the circle map θ → θ + Ω − K·sin(2πθ)/(2π) (frequency Ω to the right, K upward, every pixel iterating 240 steps for the rotation number; near a fraction p/q with the denominator up to 7 it is drawn as a locked tongue, with the tone falling with the denominator, and elsewhere as a dark background that depends on the rotation number), coral orange on a dark indigo; the seed picks one of 6 windows (the full plot, the left half, the middle half, the right half, K up to 0.6 and K from 0.5); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture in which only the tongues with the denominator up to 7 are marked; the tests read only the drawing back and check the closed-form edges |Ω| = K/(2π) of the 0/1 tongue, the locking to 1/2 along Ω = 1/2 for every K, the symmetry Ω → 1 − Ω and the agreement with a rotation number found separately in the test with 2000 steps; the colour is steep near the edges of the tongues, so the C1 sweep checks only the values whose slope is under 100; the positions and widths of the individual tongues and the fall of the tone with the denominator are not tested; the window for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the description is from memory and was not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; a 512² map took about 1.4 s in Bun (two runs, not a benchmark). Current catalog: 1109 kinds, 1110 gallery choices including plain.

- `powderdiffraction`: Powder diffraction — the Debye–Scherrer rings of X-ray powder diffraction of a cubic crystal (each allowed reflection (h, k, l) makes a ring at the Bragg angle sin θ = √(h²+k²+l²) / (2a/λ) with the radius L·tan 2θ; the brightness includes the multiplicity and the Lorentz-polarisation and Debye–Waller factors), the selection rules of the lattice deciding which rings are missing (body-centred h+k+l even, face-centred h, k, l of the same parity, and the simple cubic lattice lacks the N that are not a sum of three squares), mint green on a dark green-black; the seed picks one of 6 configurations (simple, body-centred and face-centred cubic, each with two values of 2a/λ); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture, not a simulation (no atomic scattering factors, no particle-size broadening, a fixed ring width); the tests read only the drawing back and check, with a selection-rule enumeration and Bragg's formula written separately in the test, that every allowed reflection has a ring within 0.001 of its position, that forbidden reflections have none and that the rings are circles; the ring brightness is not tested; the configuration for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas are from memory and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1108 kinds, 1109 gallery choices including plain.

- `brillouinzones`: Brillouin zones — the Bragg planes of a two-dimensional reciprocal lattice (the perpendicular bisector of every reciprocal lattice vector G) and the Brillouin zones they cut out (the zone number being 1 plus the number of lattice points closer to the point than the origin, shaded from bright to dark by zone number, the zones from the 7th on sharing one tone), rose pink on a dark purple-black; the seed picks one of 6 reciprocal lattices (square, hexagonal, rectangular, oblique, rhombic and a stretched hexagonal one); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture, not a solid-state calculation; the tests read only the drawing back and, with the zone number counted separately in the test by comparing distances, check the shading by zone number, a line on every Bragg plane and the half-turn and mirror symmetry of the lattice's point group; the theorem that all zones have equal area is not tested; the configuration for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the definition is from memory and was not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1107 kinds, 1108 gallery choices including plain.

- `mapprojections`: Map projections — the graticule (every 15°, the equator and the central meridian brighter) and Tissot indicatrices (angular radius 6°) of the unit sphere in six projections: Mercator, equirectangular, sinusoidal, Mollweide, stereographic and orthographic, as thin teal lines on a dark blue-black; the sampler has only the inverses of the projections and uses the numerical gradient of the inverse for a first-order line distance; the seed picks one of 6 projections; not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture, not a cartography program; the tests read only the drawing back and, with forward formulas written separately in the test, check that the graticule passes through predicted points, that each Tissot circle passes through the four points given by spherical geometry, the inside and outside of the map outline and the mirror symmetry about the central meridian and the equator; they do not check the amount of distortion itself; there is a band about 2.4 pixels wide with no lines at the map boundary and the orthographic edge; the configuration for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test (smallest margin 0.028), not an independent oracle; the formulas of the projections are from memory and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1106 kinds, 1107 gallery choices including plain.

- `diffractionpatterns`: Diffraction patterns — the intensity of far-field (Fraunhofer) diffraction: a single aperture (the Airy pattern (2·J₁(ρ)/ρ)² for a circular one, sinc²·sinc² for a rectangular one) times the array factor [sin(Nφ)/(N·sin φ)]² of N apertures in a row (N − 1 dark lines between neighbouring principal maxima; N = 2 is Young's double hole), cream on a dark blue-black; the seed picks one of 6 configurations (the Airy pattern at two scales, a rectangular aperture and rows of 2, 5 and 3 circular holes); not a periodic pattern; a schematic scalar far-field picture; the tests read only the drawing back and, with a power series for J₁, Newton's method for the zeros and a direct ratio of sines written in the test, check the correlation with the intensity, the first two Airy dark rings at the zeros of J₁, the zeros of the array factor inside the main lobe, the whole zero lines of the rectangular aperture, and the half-turn and mirror symmetry; the accuracy of the trapezoid integral for J₁ was not measured on its own; the later rings and the heights of the secondary maxima are not tested; the configuration for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas are from memory and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1105 kinds, 1106 gallery choices including plain.

- `errordiffusion`: Error diffusion — a 96×96-cell black-and-white dithered image (a lit cell is a pale dot, a dark one a very faint small dot) in which a grey-level source image is turned into black and white by error diffusion in raster order, on a dark blue-black; the seed picks one of 6 configurations (Floyd–Steinberg, Atkinson, Jarvis–Judice–Ninke, Stucki, Burkes and Sierra Lite on a horizontal ramp, 8 steps, a radial ramp or a diagonal ramp); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture; the tests read only the drawing back and compare cell by cell with an error diffusion written separately in the test as a pull, and check that the mean grey level is kept (Atkinson, which sends on only 3/4 of the error, deviates in the darkest and lightest strips); the weights of the kernels are from memory (their sums were checked with a small program) and were not checked against the literature; the configuration for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; construction and sampling time were not measured. Current catalog: 1104 kinds, 1105 gallery choices including plain.

- `keplerorbits`: Kepler orbits — a star in the middle (the shared focus) and four elliptical orbits, each with a bright dot at every equal time step and a spoke from each dot to the star (equal areas swept in equal times, Kepler's second law), the dots coming from Kepler's equation (Newton's method) and the semi-major axes following the third law a ∝ N^(2/3), light violet on a dark purple-black; the seed picks one of 6 configurations (the dots per period, eccentricity, direction and phase of each of the 4 orbits); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture with two-body motion only and no perturbations; the tests read only the drawing back and, with a Newton solution and a conic area integral written separately in the test, check the fit of the orbits, dots and star, a bright dot at every equal time step and equal swept areas between isolated dot pairs; the third law was not measured independently from the picture (the geometry of the test is calculated from it); the configuration for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas of the laws are from memory and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1103 kinds, 1104 gallery choices including plain.

- `zerovelocitycurves`: Zero-velocity curves — the contours of the effective potential Ω = (x² + y²)/2 + (1 − μ)/r₁ + μ/r₂ of the circular restricted three-body problem in the rotating frame (the boundaries of the Hill regions), thin golden lines on a dark teal-black, the two primaries being small discs and the brighter thicker lines being the critical contours through the saddle points L1, L2 and L3 and the contour of L4 and L5 that shrinks to points; the seed picks one of 6 mass ratios (0.5, 0.3, 0.12, Earth–Moon 0.01215, Sun–Jupiter 0.0009537 and 0.04); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture, not an orbit simulation; the line distance is a first-order approximation; the tests read only the drawing back and, with a potential, a bisection and closed forms written separately in the test, check the fit to the contours of the potential, that a bright critical contour passes through each of the five Lagrange points (the minimum 3/2 − μ(1 − μ)/2 at L4 and L5), that the number of contours across the axis equals the number of roots of the drawn levels, and the mirror symmetry about the axis; the mass ratio 0.0009537 has no check of the contours across the axis; the configuration for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas of the potential and the Lagrange points are from memory and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1102 kinds, 1103 gallery choices including plain.

- `escapefractals`: Escape fractals — the Burning Ship, a zoom near its antenna, the tricorn and the multibrot sets of power 3, 4 and 5, as a light-blue glowing gradient and contour bands (the smooth iteration count of the escape time, the inside dark) on a dark blue-black; the seed picks one of 6 configurations; not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture, not a numerically exact boundary; it is not C1 (the boundary of the set is a fractal) and the tests check smoothness only far from the set; the tests read only the drawing back and, with an independent iteration rewritten with complex numbers as objects, check that the inside/outside classification agrees, that the extent of the set on the real axis fits the closed forms (−2, 1/4, −2^(1/(d−1)), (d − 1)·d^(−d/(d−1)) and so on; the left end of the Burning Ship is not tested because of amplified rounding), the conjugation and rotation symmetry of the tricorn and the multibrots (the Burning Ship was measured to be asymmetric) and the smoothness and periodicity far from the set; the configuration for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the definitions and symmetries are from memory and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1101 kinds, 1102 gallery choices including plain.

- `fourierseries`: Fourier series — the tile is divided into 4 rows, each drawing one period of the Fourier partial sum of a square, sawtooth or triangle wave with the number of terms growing from the top row to the bottom, orange curves and dim thin horizontal lines on a dark brown-black; the square wave and the sawtooth show the Gibbs overshoot at their jumps (about 9% of the jump, only narrower with more terms); the seed picks one of 6 configurations (the waveform and the number of terms of each row); a periodic pattern; a schematic picture, the curve being a first-order distance approximation; the tests read only the drawing back and, with direct term-by-term sums in the test, check the fit to the partial-sum curves, the Gibbs overshoot of the square wave (the peak at π/(2m) and a fraction within 0.003 of the Gibbs constant calculated by numerical integration), the plateaus flattening with more terms and the symmetry of the waveform; the sawtooth and the triangle wave have only the fit and the symmetry checks, and the symmetry cannot tell the sign pattern of the sawtooth; the configuration for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the series formulas are from memory and were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1100 kinds, 1101 gallery choices including plain.

- `twosourcewaves`: Two-source waves — the time-averaged intensity cos²(π(r₁ − r₂) / wavelength) of two point sources in phase, with bright bands where the path difference is a whole number of wavelengths and dark lines (hyperbolas with the sources as foci) where it is a half-integer number, the perpendicular bisector always bright, light green on a dark green-black, the sources being small bright dots; the seed picks one of 6 configurations (the spacing in wavelengths, the wavelength and the angle of the line of sources); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture with no decay, polarisation or time dependence; the tests read only the drawing back and, with the intensity written in the test, check the correlation with it, that a circle has exactly 4M dark runs (M = ⌈spacing − ½⌉), that the dark lines on the line of sources are at ±(m + ½)·wavelength / 2, that the perpendicular bisector is fully bright, and the symmetry under swapping the sources and the mirror symmetry of the axis-aligned configurations; the configuration for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formula was not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1099 kinds, 1100 gallery choices including plain.

- `fieldlines`: Field lines — the field lines (contours of Σ q·atan2, 12 lines per unit charge, as in Gauss's law) and the dimmer equipotentials (contours of Σ q·ln r) of several line charges in the plane, pink lines on a dark purple-black, the charges being small discs (the sign is not shown); the seed picks one of 6 configurations of charges (a dipole, an equal pair, 2 : −1 : −1, a quadrupole, three equal charges and four alternating ones); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture, not an electromagnetic simulation; the line distance is a first-order approximation; the tests read only the drawing back and, with a field and angle sum written in the test, check the field-line fit, that exactly 12|q| lines leave each charge (Gauss's law), that the lines run in the direction of the field, mirror symmetry and that a line goes through a null point; the configuration for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the formulas for charges in two dimensions were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1098 kinds, 1099 gallery choices including plain.

- `cylinderflow`: Cylinder flow — the ideal potential flow from left to right past a unit cylinder with circulation 4π·g, pale-blue streamlines (contours of ψ = y(1 − 1 / r²) + 2g ln r at a spacing of 0.2) with one thicker dividing streamline, the cylinder drawn as a dark disc with a bright rim; the seed picks one of 6 circulations g = 0, 0.5, 1, 1.25, 1.5 and 0.75 (for g > 1 the stagnation points leave the cylinder); not a periodic pattern; a schematic picture and not a fluid simulation; the line distance is a first-order approximation, with 1×10⁻⁴ added to the squared speed near stagnation points to keep it C1; the tests read only the drawing back and, with a stream function and velocity written separately from polar form, check that the streamlines fit, that the lines run in the direction of the velocity, left-right mirror symmetry (top-bottom only for g = 0) and that the dividing streamline passes through the stagnation points; the circulation for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the potential-flow formulas were not checked against the literature; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1097 kinds, 1098 gallery choices including plain.

- `sphericalharmonics`: Spherical harmonics — the tile is divided into 2 × 4 rectangular maps, each a longitude-latitude map of a real spherical harmonic Y_l^m (tone 0.5 + 0.5·Y / the largest value, nodal lines as the middle grey, a thin frame), pale green on dark green-black; the seed picks one of 6 starts (8 harmonics taken in the order of l and m); |m| nodal meridians and l − |m| nodal parallels; unlike `zernikemodes` (Zernike polynomials on discs); the tests read only the drawing back and, with an independent formula by differentiating the explicit sum of the Legendre polynomial, check the rank correlation, the number of nodal lines and the positions of the zeros of the zonal harmonics; the start for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the sign convention was not compared with a textbook; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1096 kinds, 1097 gallery choices including plain.

- `wallpapergroups`: Wallpaper groups — the tile is divided into 2 × 2 unit cells and a small motif with no symmetry of its own (a hook and a dot) is repeated in each under one of the 12 plane groups that a square lattice allows (p1, p2, pm, pg, cm, pmm, pmg, pgg, cmm, p4, p4m, p4g), golden lines on a dark blue-black; the seed picks a group; the symmetry group of the picture is exactly the chosen group; a periodic pattern; the 5 groups of a hexagonal lattice are not included; the tests read only the drawing back: the orders of the groups from composing generators and compared with the operation lists, exactly the operations of the group leaving 32 candidate operations unchanged, and the number of 4-connected components being 2 × the order × 4; the group for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the names of the groups rest on memory and the International Tables were not checked; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1095 kinds, 1096 gallery choices including plain.

- `mapattractors`: Planar map attractors — the middle of the tile shows the density of the orbit of a planar chaotic map (Clifford and de Jong): 500 thousand points spread bilinearly over a 384 by 384 grid, blurred once and turned into a tone by a logarithm, rose pink on dark purple-black; the seed picks one of 6 variants; not a periodic pattern; unlike `lorenzattractor` (one trajectory line of a continuous flow) and `logisticbifurcation` (the bifurcation diagram of a one-dimensional map); the grid is interpolated with smoothstep weights, so it is C1; the tests read only the drawing back and, with the cell counts of an independent orbit (another start and length), check the density correlation, the dark areas and the extent box; the variant for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the pixels are not guaranteed to be bit-for-bit the same across JavaScript engines (the precision of sin and cos is up to the engine); an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; building a sampler took about 125 to 195 ms (single measurement with Bun). Current catalog: 1094 kinds, 1095 gallery choices including plain.

- `htree`: H-tree — one H-tree fractal in the middle of the tile: each of the 4 ends of an H (a bar of length L with an upright bar of length L at each end) grows an H of size r·L, turned a quarter turn, n generations being drawn and the ends of the last generation getting small dots; r = 0.5 is the classic H-tree; fine ice-blue lines on a dark blue-black; the seed picks one of 6 variants [generations, ratio, turned]; one figure in the middle of the tile, so not a periodic pattern; unlike `pythagorastree` (glazed square tiles); the segment kernels are combined as `1 − Π(1 − t²)`, so there are no creases; the tests read only the drawing back and, with the segments of an explicit construction by generations (without pruning), check the segment points, the distance of bright pixels to the segments and dots, and the end dots; the variant for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the construction is the same definition as the design; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not formally measured. Current catalog: 1093 kinds, 1094 gallery choices including plain.

- `cornuspirals`: Cornu spirals — the tile is divided into n × n cells, each with one Cornu spiral (the Euler spiral, the clothoid, curvature proportional to arc length) with a bright dot at the inflection point in the middle and a tightening scroll at each end, fine amber lines on a dark purple-black; the seed picks one of 6 variants [cells, T, turn, mirror], odd cells (checkerboard) being mirrored and then turned a quarter turn; the tile has a whole number of cells, so it is a periodic pattern; unlike `harmonograph` (one large figure of two damped pendulums), `conformalspiral` and `sacksspiral`; the distance is to the curve itself (Newton steps), not to a polyline; the tests read only the drawing back and, with the power series of the Fresnel integrals (a different calculation from the sampler's Simpson table), check the curve points, the distance of bright pixels to the curve, that the cells repeat under the turn and mirror and the dot at the centre; the variant for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not formally measured. Current catalog: 1092 kinds, 1093 gallery choices including plain.

- `catenaryfestoons`: Catenary festoons — in each row chains drop between equally spaced pegs as catenaries y = a·(cosh(c) − cosh(s / a)) (the sag at the middle is a·(cosh(c) − 1)), fine teal lines and bright pegs on a dark blue-black; the seed picks one of 6 variants [spans, rows, c, staggered], odd rows being shifted by half a span when staggered; the tile has a whole number of spans and rows, so it is a periodic pattern; unlike the existing `scallopedslate`, `fishscaletiles` and `fishscaleshingles` (overlapping shingle surfaces with semicircular lower edges) these are thin lines on a flat ground, the curves being catenaries, with no overlap or shadows; the tests read only the drawing back: peg positions computed by separate code, no chain at the middle of a span, the middle line of the chain on the catenary written with exp (a parabola can be told apart only for variants 1, 3 and 5) and a brute-force polyline distance; the variant for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; the distance is a first-order approximation; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1091 kinds, 1092 gallery choices including plain.

- `logisticbifurcation`: Bifurcation diagram of the logistic map — x → r·x·(1 − x) with r along the horizontal axis, each column iterated 300 times and then the next 120 values drawn, x vertical: the fixed-point curve x = 1 − 1 / r, the period-doubling at r = 3, the chaotic bands and the periodic windows (the period-3 window at r about 3.83), the edges of the chaotic bands being r²(4 − r) / 16 and r / 4, fine cream dots on dark brown-black with a rectangular frame; the seed picks one of 6 ranges (the whole diagram and several enlargements); not a periodic pattern; the tests read only the drawing back and, with an independent iteration (written differently and using only columns whose orbit has settled), check the stable cycle points, that there are no extra bright dots, the bounds of the orbit and the closed forms of the fixed point and the 2-cycle; the chaotic bands are checked only by the bounds; r is taken at the middle of one of 2048 column steps, so the picture jumps at the boundaries of the steps along u (a lot in the chaotic bands), and continuity along u is not claimed; the range for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1090 kinds, 1091 gallery choices including plain.

- `zordercurve`: Z-order (Morton) curve — the 2^b × 2^b cells of a grid (4×4, 8×8, 16×16) sorted by the index made by interleaving the bits of the column and the row, the centres of cells with consecutive indices joined by straight segments, which repeat a "Z" at every scale and have big jumps (it is not a continuous space-filling curve), the tone of the nodes rising along the curve from dark to light, magenta on dark purple; the seed picks one of 6 pairs (b, transposed); unlike `hilbertcurve`, `peanocurve` and `moorecurve` it keeps the interleaved-bit structure and has jumps; the tests read only the drawing back and build the Morton order independently from binary strings, check the middles of the segments, the dark places and the tones of the nodes by index; the minimum distance near the nodes may have creases and C1 near the nodes is not claimed; the variant for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; sets 2 and 3 were not viewed in previews; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured (every pixel scans all the segments, with no spatial index). Current catalog: 1089 kinds, 1090 gallery choices including plain.

- `polygonoffsets`: Polygon offsets — a polygon in the middle of the disc (a five-pointed star, a six-pointed star, an L, a triangle, a cross, an arrow) with a line at every fixed step of the signed distance to it (circular arcs at the corners outside, sharp corners on the medial axis inside) and the bands between the lines in four tones by inside or outside and the parity of the band number, thin green lines on dark green; the seed picks one of 6 polygons; unlike `topographicmap` (contours of a sinusoidal terrain) and `kaleidoscope` (iso-bands of an averaged noise) it is the geometric distance to a polygon; the tests read only the drawing back and compute the distance by brute force (the least distance over points along the sides, the winding number for inside or outside), check the lines and bands by a forward construction, check that the bright line pixels are at whole multiples of the step and check the tones of the bands; **there are folds on the medial axis inside and the cut locus outside, which are not C1**, and the C1 sweep covers only points that avoid them; the vertices come from the preset and the polygon for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1088 kinds, 1089 gallery choices including plain.

- `powerdiagram`: Power diagram (Laguerre–Voronoi diagram) — the tile is divided into c × c cells with one disc in each (jittered centre, different radii, not touching), and the whole tile is divided into polygonal cells by the power |p − c|² − r² of a point about a circle, the cell edges lying on the radical axes of two circles (straight; with equal radii it is the ordinary Voronoi diagram, with different radii the edges move towards the smaller circle), thin coral lines on dark wine, the inside of the discs in two random tones; the seed picks one of 6 pairs (c, spread); unlike `voronoicells` (nearest-site colour plates, soft weights) the cells here are decided by the power of discs with radii, the edges are radical axes and the discs themselves are drawn; drawn pixel by pixel by scanning the powers of the surrounding sites; the tests read only the drawing back, find the discs in the picture and check independently the number and roundness of the discs and the distance of the bright line pixels from the line of equal power (and that it is not the Voronoi bisector); the positions and radii are estimated from the picture, so the residuals include pixel quantisation; the distance function has a crease near the vertices of the cells and is not C1 there; the variant for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test and also checked against the picture; set 2 was not viewed in previews; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1087 kinds, 1088 gallery choices including plain.

- `reuleauxfield`: Reuleaux polygon field — the tile is divided into c × c cells, each with a Reuleaux polygon in its middle (a curve of constant width with an odd number n of corners: the intersection of n discs centred at the corners with the distance between opposite corners as the radius, the same width in every direction), its width 0.8 of the cell and a random turn, thin teal lines on dark teal-black, the inside filled with two alternating tones by the parity of the sum of the cell's column and row; the seed picks one of 6 pairs (n, c) ((3, 4), (3, 5), (5, 4), (5, 3), (7, 3), (7, 4)); drawn pixel by pixel from the distance to each arc; the tests read only the drawing back and do not use the construction: the connected regions are taken as the shapes and they check the number of shapes, equal widths in 180 directions, an area agreeing with the textbook Reuleaux polygon (within 2%), the corner ratio, the centroid at the cell middle and the tones alternating with the parity of column plus row; what is compared is the shape after the lines have eaten the rim, so the tolerances are loose; the variant for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test but is also checked against the number of shapes and the corner ratio read from the picture; sets 1 and 5 were not viewed in previews; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1086 kinds, 1087 gallery choices including plain.

- `eisensteinprimes`: Eisenstein primes — of the Eisenstein integers a + bω (norm N = a² − ab + b²) on the triangular lattice, those with a norm up to the square of the radius are drawn as dots, the primes (a prime norm, or a unit times a rational prime = 2 mod 3) as bright dots (dimmer farther from the origin) and the others as very small dim dots, lilac on dark purple, with six-fold rotational symmetry; the seed picks one of 6 radii (R = 8 to 18); unlike `gaussianprimes` (a square grid, four-fold symmetry); drawn pixel by pixel from the nearest lattice point; the tests read only the drawing back and check independently, by a brute-force search for factors (a divisibility criterion from multiplying by the conjugate, different from the rule of the sampler), whether each lattice point is a prime, and check the invariance under a 60-degree turn and that the middles of neighbouring pairs read as the ground; the formula for the lattice positions and the preset data are shared by both sides, and the radius for each seed was measured by a probe and written into the test, not an independent oracle; set 2 (R = 12) was not viewed in previews; an authored schematic with no specific source cited and no photograph compared. Defaults: 0.5 m tile, 0.0008 UV relief (not metres), metallic 0; sampling time was not measured. Current catalog: 1085 kinds, 1086 gallery choices including plain.
